New Delhi, the 16th October, 1979 

S.O, 3567. — ^In pursuance of sub-section (1) of Section 8 
of the Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes Orders (Amend- 
ment) Act, 1976, the following Order made by the Election 
Commission under clause (d) of sub-section (3) of Section 6 
of the Act is hereby published : — 

ORDER 

Whereas under clause (a) of sub-section (3) of section 6 
of the Scheduled Casies and Scheduled Tribes Orders (Amend- 
ment) Act, 1976, the proposals of the Election Commission 
to amend the Delimitation of Parliamentary and Assembly 
Constituencies Order, 1976, were published in the Gazette of 
India and in the Official Gazette of the State of Madhya Pra- 
desh on 9th September, 1977 ; 

And whereas all objections and suggestions received in 
relation to the said proposals relating to the State of Madhya 
Pradesh were considered by the Commission at fcublic 
sittings held at Bhopal on 17th September, 1979 ; at Jabalpur 
On 18th September, 1979 ; and at Chhsttarpur on 19th Sep- 
tember, 1979 ; 

The Commission hereby makes the following amendments 
in the Delimitation of Parliamentary and Assembly Consti- 
tuencies Order, 1976 (without altering the extent of any 
constituency as given in such Order) in respect of the State 
of Madhya Pradesh. — 

I. In Schedule I of the said Order, against item 

lO-Madhya Pradesh, 

(i) Under column 6, the fi^ro “6*’ shall be substituted 

for the figure "5” ; and 

(ii) Under column 7, the figure "9” shall be substituted 

for the figure “8". 

TI. In Schedule II of the said Order, against Kem 

10-Madhya Pradesh, 

(i) Under column 6, the figure "44’’ shall be substitu- 

ted for the figure “42" ; and 

(ii) Under column 7, the figure “75" shall be 

substituted for the figure "M’*. 

HI. In Schedule XII of the said Order — 

(1) In Part A — Parliamentary Constituencies —

S.O. 3S70.— 11 is hereby notilieJ for general information 
that the insliuuion mentioned below has been approved by 
the Secretary, the Department of Science & Technology, 
Neu' Delhi, the prescribed authority for the purposes of 
chiiise (ii) of sub-section ( 1 ) of Seclion 35 of the Incomc- 
(a\ Act, 1961 read with Rule 6{iv) of the Income Tax Rules, 
1962 under the .category ‘Tirstiiutiou" in the area of other 
natural or appUeil sciences, subject to the following condi- 
tions : — 

(i) that the Hyderabad Science Society, Hyderabad, will 
maintain u separate account of the sums received 
by it for scientific research in the field of natural 
Or applied sciences other than agriculture /animal 
husbandry /fialreries & medicines ; 

Oi) that the said Society will furnish the annual re- 
turn of its scientific research activities to the pres- 
cribed authority for every financial year in such 
forms as may be laid down and intimated to them 
for this purpose, by 30th April, each year. 

INSTITUTION 

Hyderabad Science Society, Hyderabad 

Thii notillcation is effective for a period of 3 years from

Whereas the Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part 
thereof stands transferred by this Notification from one range 
to another range, appeals arising out of the assessments made 
in that Income-tax Circle, Ward or Districts or part thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this notiflentiorr before 


the Appellate Assistant Commissioner of the Range from whom 
that Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part there of is 
trarisferrcd shall from the date of this notification takes effect 
be transferred to and dealt wrth by the Appellate Assrstant 
Corararssiorwr of the Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or 
District or part thereof is transferred. 

This notification shall take effect from 1-6-1979.

INCOME-TAX 

S.O. 3596, — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of Section 121 A of the Income-tax Act, 1961 (43 of 
1961) and in superses.sion of all previous notifications, the 
Central Board of Direct Taxes, hereby directs that the Commis- 
sioners of Income-tax (Appeals) of the Charges specified in 
column (1) of the Schedule below, shall perform their functions 
in respect of such persons assessed to income-tax or surtax or 
interest-tax in the Income-tax Wards Circles. Districts and 
Ranges specified in the corresponding entries in columns (2) 
and column (3) thereof as are aggrieved by any of the orders 
mentioned in clau.ses (a) to (h) of sub-section (2) of Section 246 
of the Income-tax Act, 1961, in sub-section (1 ) of Section II of 
Companies (Profits) Surtax Act, 1964 (7 of 1964), and in sub- 
section (1) of Section 15 of the Interest Tax Act, 1974 (45 of 
1974) and also in respect of such persons or classes of persons 
as the Board has directed or may direct in future in accordance 
with the provisions of clause (i) of sub-section (2) of Section 246 
of the Income-tax Act, 1961.

Now Delhi, the 16th July, 1979. 

INCOME-TAX 

S, O. 3597. —In exorcise of tho powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of Section 121 A of the Income-tax Act, 1961 (43 of 
1961) and in a supersession of its Notification No. 2382(F.No. 
261/8/7-rTJ) dated 7-7-1978 as amended from time to time the 
Central Board of Direct Taxes, hereby directs that the Commis- 
sioners of Income-tax (Appeals) of the Charges specified in 
column (1) of the Schedule below, shall perform their functions 
in respect of such persons assessed to income-tax or surtax or 
interest-tax in the Income-tax Wards, Circles, Districts and 
Ranges specified in the corresponding entries in column (2) 

717 G of 1 / 79—3 


and column (3) thereof as are aggrieved by any of the orders 
mentioned in clauses (a) to (h) of sub-section (2) of Section 246 
of the Income-tax Act, 1961, in sub-section (1) of Section 11 of 
Companies (Profits) Surtax Act, 1964 (7 of 1964), and in sub- 
section (1) of Section 15 of the Interest tax Act, 1974 (45 of 1974) 
and also in respect of such persons or classes of persons as the 
Board has directed or may direct in future in accordance with 
tha provisions of clausa (i) of Sub-section (2) of Section 246 of 
tho Income-tax Act, 1961.

thereof stands transferred by this notification from one Range 
to another Range, appeals arising out of the assessments made 
in that Income-tax Circle, Ward or Distt. or part thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this notification before 
the Appellate Asstt. Commissioner of the Range from whom 
that Income-tax Circle, Ward or Distt, or part thereof is 
transferred shall from the date of this notification takes effect 
be transferred to and dealt with by the Appellate Asstt. 
Commissioner of the Ran^ to whom the said Circle, Ward 
or District or part thereof is transferred.

S.O. 3605.“ Whereas by the notification of Government of India as shown in the schedule appended hereto and issued under sub- 
section (ii) of section 6 of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) Act, 1962, the Right of User 
has been acquired in the lands specified in the schedule appended thereto for the Indian Oil Corporation Limited for the transport of 
petroleum from Salaya in Gujarat State to Mathura in Uttar Pradesh and from Viramgam to Gujarat Refinery. Koyali, in Giyarat 
Slate. 

And whereas the Indian Oil Corporation Limited has terminated the operation referred to in clause (i) of sub-section (1) of section 
7 of the said Act on the date shown against the name of village in the schedule. 

Now therefore, under rule 4 of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) Rules, 1963, the 
Competent Authority hereby notifies the said date as the date of termination of the said operation.

tional General Manager" shall be inserted.

New Delhi, the 18th October, 1979 

S.O. 3627. — Whereas a public notice lor revi.siiig the 
local urea of Coimbatore, Sulur and Pcrianaikcnapalayam 
Telephone Exchange System was published as required by 
rule 434(111) (bb) of the Indian Telegraph Rules, 1951 in 
the Newspapers in circulation at Coknbatore, Sulur and 
Perianalkcnpalayain inviting objections and suggestions from 
all persons likely to be affected thereby, within a period of 
.30 days from the date of publication of the notice in the 
Ncw.spapcrs ; 

And whcrea.s the said notice was made available to the 
public on 18-6-79 and 19-6-1979 in Tamil Daily “Malai 
Muiasu" on 18-6-1979 and 19-6-1979 in Tail Daily "Daily 
Thanthi”, on 20-6-1979 and 21-6-1979 in English Doily “The 
Hindu’’ and on 20-6-1979 and 21-6-1979 English Daily "Indian 
Express’' ; 


l.S'h/TqfffTt 5, .1901 


And whereas objections and suggestions received from the 
public on the said notice have been considered by the Central 
tjovemment ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by rule 
434(111) (bb) of the said Rules, the Director General, Posts 
and lelegniphs hereby declares that with effect from 1-11-79 
the revised local area of Coimbatore, Sulur and Perianaiken- 
palayam shall be as under ; 

1. Coimbtore Telephone Exchange System 

The local area of Coimbatore Telephone system shall coyer 
an area falling under the jurisdiction of Coimbatore Munici- 
pality (A.s existing on the date of Notification), provided 
further that the telephone subscribers located outside Coim- 
batore municipality but who arc served from Coimbatore 
telephone system will continue to pay local tariffs as long 
as they arc located within 5 KMs of any exchange of this 
system and remain connected to it. 

2. Siihir Telephone Exchange System 

The local area of Sulur Telephone system shall cover an 
area falling within 5 KMs radial distance from Sulur Tele- 
phone Exchange, 

3. Pcrianaikenapalayam Telephone Exchange System 

The local area of Perianaikenapalayam Telephone System 
shall cover an area falling within 5 KMs radial distance from 
Perianaikenapalayam Telephone Excharnge.

AWARD 

On an industrial dispute hat arose between the Manage- 
ment of Sri Kalyanarania Mica Mine, Kalichedu, Rapur 
Taluk in Nellore District, and their workmen, the Ciovern- 
ment of India by its order da‘ed 21-2-1978 referred the 
matters specified in the Schedule, to this Tribunal for 
adjudicatoin under Section 10(1 1(d) of the Induslria'l Dis- 
putes Act, 1947. 

SCHEDULE 


"Whether Ihc Management of Sree Kalyana Rama 
Mica Mine, is juj.stificd in terminating the services 
of Sri Manda Thcllaiah, Driller and Malll Venkata- 
swamy, underground ma'zdoor with effect from 
13-7-1977 ? If so, what relief they -are entitled to?” 


2, The facts that gave rise to this reference, briefly stated 
are the following : — Sri Manda Tallalah and Sri Malli 
Venkataswamy, who shall hereafter be referred to m the 
Petitioners or workmen, were ernployed in the service of 
Sri Kalyana Rama Mica Mines, Kalichedu, in Rapur Taluk 
of Nellore District, a.s Driller and Underground Mazdoor 
(Mucker) respectively. Alleging that both tjie abovemen- 
tioned workmen, who were discharged from service on 
medical grounds, after paying Silicosis compensation in the 
years 1964 and 1962 respectively, reioined service fa thc| 
names of Manda Thallaiah and Malli Venkutaiah, whereas 
their real names wci'c Manda Venkatasiibbaiah^ and Malli 
Venkatswamv respectively, with a view to avoid detection 
of their having worked previously which the management 
however found out during 1977, the services of the above 
two workmen were terminated with effect from 13-7-1977. 
Aggrieved by the action taken by the Management, both 
the workmen rafsed a dispute questioning the validity of the 
termination and efforts at conciliation having failed, the 
Ciovemment of India, afier sati.sfying itself about the exis- 
tence of an industrial dispute between the parties, referred 
the same to this Tribunal for adjudication. 


3. The case of the petitioners as disclosed in the claim 
statement filed on their behalf is the following 

4. Both the petitioners were employed in the service of 
the Respondent-management and had put in more than 15 
years of service by 1977. The Management of Sri Kalyana 
Rama Mica Mines, has been in the habit of retrenching 
some of the workmen periodically in order to deprive the 
workmen from having the benefit of continuous service and 
the resultant benefits. In October 1976 both these petitioners, 
among others were retrenched for no valid reason and 
some new persons were recruited in the place of those 
retrenched workmen. This unreasonable action of the raange- 
ment was questioned by the workmen through their union 
and in the presence of the Labour Enforcement Officer 
(Central) Gudur, an agreement was reached between the 
Management and the Union on 6-6-1977 under which, the 
management agreed to take back certain retrenched workers, 
including those two petitioners into service. .Accordingly 
these petitioners were taken ba-ck Into service 
on 8-6-1977. Inapite of it the management, once again 
terminated the service of both these petitioners on L3-7-1977 
and this time, on the ground (hat the Petitioners, who were 
found to be suffering from Silico.sis even in 1962-64 were 
discharged from service after paying compensation and that 
they cannot therefore be retained in service any longer. 
This ground urged by the Management is false as at no 
time the pelltkmers were got examined by the Medical 


5. The Respondent Management has filed a counter con- 
tending as follows : It is true that both the Petitioners who 
were retrenched in October 1976 were taken back into 
service in pursuance of an agreement reached between the 
parties in the presence of the Labour Enforcement Officer. 
After they were so taken back into service, Malli Venkates- 
wamy claimed that he, ht.'d worked in the Mines even pre- 
viously now and then. The management than verified the 
records and found that no one by name Malli Venkataswamy 
worked at any time previously. However, it was noticed 
that one Malli Venkataiab had worked now and then pre- 
viously. That person who worked as Malli Venkataiab was 
found to be no other than MaUi Venkataswamy who left 
the service after taking Silicosis compensation on 29-9-1962 
but had managed to rejoin service a few years later, in the 
assumed name as MwUi Venkataiab. Similarly, Manda Thal- 
laiah whose real name is Manda Venkata Subbiah and who 
loo had left the service after taking Silicosis compensation 
on 8-10-1964 appears to have rejoined service in the assu- 
med name of Manda Thallaiah. This fraud played by the 
patitioners could not Immediately be noticed by the Mine 
Manager as he too joined the service only in 1967. Soon 
after the fraud was detected a show cause notice was given to 
the Petitioners on 7-7-1977. Malli Venkataswamy in his 
reply admitted having received Silicosis compensation but 
tried to justify his action in rejoin service in a different 
name by offering some lame excuses. The explanation given 
by Manda Thellalah was wholly untenable. Therefore, the 
management was left with no option but to terminate their 
services. Since the termination affected was for good cause 
f.e. to prevent occupational health hazards to the petitioners, 
as well as the other workmen, the same is perfectly legal 
and cannot be questioned. The petitioners arc not therefore 
entitled to any relief. 


6, Now that the fwet of both the petitioners herein having 
been removed from service with effect from 13-7-1977 with- 
out holding any enquiry i.s not in dispute, the Management 
obviously wanted to justify its action by leading evidence 
before this Tribunal. The only ground on which the peti- 
tioners were removed from service is that both of them 
were found to be suffering from silicosis, which is an occu- 
pational disease even in the years 1962-64 and that having 
left service after receiving silicosis compensation they mana- 
ged to rejoin service in different names, obviously with a 
view to suppress the fact of their being silicosis patients. 
This is denied by the petitioners in their counter. 


/. In proof of its case that both the petitioners were dis- 
charged from service after paying silicosis compensation and 
that they managed to rejoin service in different names which 
was detected only in 1977, the agent of the Respondent- 
Management Is examined as M.W. 1 through whom the 
dociunents filed are marked as Exs. M 1 to M 7(a). O^t 
of the two workmen on whose behalf this dispute has been 
raised, only Malli Venkataswamy is examined as W.W. 1 
and the documents filed by him are markrf as Exs. W 1 to 
W 6. The other workman by name Manda Thcllaiah did 
not appear nor any evidence is adduced on his beh^f. 


8. M.W. 1 has deposed that both the workman were found 
to be suffering from silicosis, that therefore they were dis- 
charged from service afler paying silicosis compensation that 
they somehow managed to rejoin service in assumed names 
suppressing their real names and that therefore, they were 
again removed from service with effect from 13-7-1977. 
•According to M. W. I the real name of Manda Thcllaiah is 
MauBxIa Vcnkatastibhiah, in which name he worked in the 
mine as a Drillei"; before he was discharged from service on 
medical grounds m the year 1964. Ex. M 4 is the Medical 
certificate issued by Dr. Ramachandra Rao, to the said Manda 
Venkata SubbaSah which is to the effect that he was suffer- 
ing from silicosis which resulted in permanent partial dis- 
ability assessed at 15 per cent. Because of this, he was paid 



[Part II — Sec. 3(ii)] 


3022 the gazette of INDIA ; OCTOBER 27, 1979/KARTIKA 5, 1901 


fl conipcnsRtioD of Rs. 882.00 on 8-10-1964 before discharg- 
ing him from service. It is further the evidence of M. W. 1 
that the said Manda Venka n Subbaiah managed to rejoin 
service in the assumed name of Ma'nda Thellai;ih which was 
detected in Inly 1977 due to which he was removed from 
service. This portion of M. W. S's evidence has not been 
contraverted either by cross-examining M, W. 1 or by lead- 
ing evidence on behalf of Manda Thellaiah. Thus the evidence 
of M. W. 1 so far as Manda TheUaiah is concerned stands 
unrebutted and the sa-me has to therefore be accepted. Once 
the evindence of M.W. 1 is accepted, the inevitable conclu- 
sion is that Manda Thellaiah who previously worked in the 
name of Manda Venka asubbiah was proved to be a silicosis 
patient, that he was therefore discharged from service a-ftcr 
paying him silicosis compensation and that he somehow 
managed to rejoin service in the assumed name of Manda 
Thellaiah. This conduct of his amounts to misconduct. 
Moreover, Silico.sis being an occupational disea'se, it is not 
desirable to keep such persons in sei vice, particularly in coal 
mines. I therefore find that the Resixmdcnt-Managemcnt is 
justified in terminating the service of Manda Thellaiah as 
a consequence of which ho will not be entitled to any 
relief. 


9. There remains the case of Malli Venkataswamy, whose 
services also were terminated for the very same reason for 
which Manda Thellaiah was removed from service. Since 
the Management hag come forward with an allegation that 
this workman also rejoined aervico in the assumed name of 
Malli Venkataiah after being discliarged from service on 
medical giounds, I shall first consider how far this allegation 
made by the Management is true. 


10. 1 may at the outset state that the Management has 
not taken a specific stand in this regard in the counter filed. 
In para 2 of the counter it is pleaded that on verification of 
the previous record, it wa,s found that no one by name Malli 
Venkataswamy worked previously and that only one person 
by name Malli Venkataiah had worked previously. But in 
para 4 of the counter, it is alleged that Malli Venkataswamy 
rejoined service in the name of Malli Venkataiah. If no 
person bv name Venkataswamy had worked previously but 
only one person by name Venkataiah had worked as pleaded 
in para 2 of the counter question of his rejoining service in 
the assumed name of Venkataiah does not at all arise and 
in this view, the plea taken in para 4 of the counter is in- 
consistent with the plea taken in para 2. 


11. The evidence of M. W. 1, in this regard is equally 
inconsistent. He no doubt stated that Malli Venkataswamy 
having worked in that name previously, rejoined service in 
August, 1976 in the name of Venkata'iah. But this part of 
his evidence is not consistent with the documentary evidence 
produced in this regard. Ex. W 4 fs the notice dated 5-7-1977 
served on Malli Venkataswamy asking him to .show cause 
why action shall not bo taken against him for having re- 
joined service in the assumed name of Venkataiah. If he 
had rejoined service as Venkataiah, in the notice covered by 
Ex. W 4 he shouUl have been described as Venkataiah alone 
and not as Venkataswamy. The notice is addressed to Ven- 
kataswamy, M. W. 1 sought to explain this by stating that 
in the notice he was desenbed a.s Venkataswamy and not as 
Venkataiah only because his real name is Venkataswamy fn 
which name he worked previously. To substantiate this piece 
of M.W. I’s evidence, reliance was also placed on the muster 
roll marked as Ex. M 7 in which this workman is described 
as Venkataiah son of Venkataswamy against S. No. 1218. 
This entry is Ex. M 7(a). As per this entry, the said 

Venkataiah was discharged from .service on 10-8-1976. If 
so, the evidence of M. W. 1 that he rejojined service in the 
name of Venkataiah in August, 1976 having previously worked 
in the name of Venkataswamy cannot be true. This apart, 
it fs admitted by M. W. 1 that no person other than Ihi-s Ven- 
kataiah was working in the Mines in the name 

of Venkataswamy by the date of settlement covered by Ex. W. I 
This settlement was reached on 7-8-1976. This shows that 
Malli Venkataswamy who was retrenched earlier had been 
re-employed even by the date of settlement and that he along 
with 12 others were agreed to be retained in service. This 
makes it clear that this workman who admittedly worked in 
the name of Venkataswamy previously rejoined service in 
the same name and not as Venkataiah, When M.W. 1 was 


confronted with this, he should not offer any explanation 
except seeking shelter under the guise of failure of his memory 
in this regard. Reliance was no doubt sought to be placed 
on Ex. M 5 which is said to bo an apphcation purported to 
have been made by Venkataswamy in the name of Venka- 
taiah in pursuance of which, and appointment order marked 
ns Ex. M 6 was issued to him. Ex. M 5 does not bear any 
date and Ex. M6 shows that the appointment order was issued 
on 15-6-1976 which was to last till 31-8-1976. This again 
is inconsfslent with the evidence of M. W. 1 abcording to 
whom, this workmirn was apiwmted in August, 1976. More- 
over even in the order by which this workman was removed 
from .service w.e.f. 13-7-1977 which is Ex. W 5 he is des- 
cribed as Venkataswamy and not ag Venkataiah. All these 
would leave no room to doubt that there is absolutely no 
truth in Ihe stand taken by the management that this work- 
man who had previously worked in the name of Venkata- 
swamy rejojined service in the name of Venkataiah. The 
workman who got himself examined as M. W. I has cate- 
gorically denied the fact of his rejoining service in the name 
of Venkataiah. On the other hand he staled that the re- 
joined service at the instance of the Raja who is the owner 
of the Mine, in the same name in which he woi ked previously 
and that if the Management had wrongly entered his name 
as Venkataiah, in the employment register, he cannot be 
blamed. On a consideration of the evidence both oral and 
documentary, I am convinced that the allegation made by 
the management to the effect that Malli Venkataswamy re- 
joined service in the name of Venkataiah is not true. 


12. It remains to be seen if this Malli Venkataswamy also 
was found to be suffering from sih'cosis, and was discharged 
from service after paying him silicosis compensation and if so 
whether the management can be compell^ to take him back 
into service despite the fact that his services were terminated 
wilhout holding any enquiry or paying him retrenchment 
compensation. 


13. It is the evidence of M. W. 1 that this Malli Venkata- 
swamy was found to so suffering from silicosis on medcial 
examination and that he was paid silicosis compensation be- 
fore discharging him from service on that ground. Ex. M 2 
is the medical certificate Issued on 25-8-1962 by Dr. Rama- 
chandra Rao to the effect that Malli Venkataswamy was found 
to be having silicosis and the permanent disablement was 
assessed at 15 per cent, Seeing on the above certificate, 
he was discharged from service after paying him a sum of 
Rs. 378.00 on 29-9-1962 as can be seen from Ex. M. 3. 
Even the workman, in his evidence has admitted In un- 
equivocal terms that he was sent for medical examination 
und that he was also paid compensation before discharging 
him from service on medical grounds. He also admitted 
'hat Ex. M. 2 is the medical certificate while Ex. M 3 is 
the receipt obtained from him for payment of comnensa- 
tion. In the light of his specific admission there can bo no 
hesitation to hold that Malli Venkataswamy was found to 
be suffering from silicosis and that he was paid compensa- 
tion when he was discharged from service. 


14. Indeed, he rejoined service and woiked for a consi- 
derable period before his sci-vfces were again terminated 
which lead to this reference. Would it give him a right to 
be retained in service is the question that remains for consi- 
deration, It is pertinent to note that the workman has not 
chosen to adduce any evidence to show that he has regained 
his normal health or that silicosis is a curable disease. It 
cannot be disputed that silicosis is an occupational disease 
and that It is neither desirable nor advisable to allow such 
person to work in mines. While so, the action laken by the 
rnana-gement in terminating his .service cannot be found to be 
illegal or unjustified. So the workmen will not be entitled 
to the relief of reinstatement. However, having regard to 
the fact that he worked for a considerable period, though 
1 am not able to specify the exact period for which he 
worked before his services were terminated. I think it just 
and reasonable to award a sum of Rs. 500.00 by way of 
compensation to him. 


15. In the result, an award is pased finding that the Manage- 
ment of Sree Kalyana Rama Mica Mine, Kalichcdu, is ju.sti- 
fied in terminating the services of Sri Manda Thellaiah and 
Malli Venkataswamy, as a consequence of which the former 
rs not entitled to any relief, while the later viz., Sri Malli

3023 


Venknttiswamy is entitled to n compciLsation of R,s. 500.00 
which the Management shall pay him within a month from 
the dale of the publication of this award.

S.O, 3635,. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act. 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
nereby publishes the following arbitration award of the Assistant 
Igibour Commissioner (Central), Hazaribagh in the industrial 
dispute between the employers in relation to the management 
of Messrs Dugda Rural Commercial Enterprises at and Post 
Office Dugda, District Giridih, Contractors of Dugda Coal 
Washery of Steel Authority of India Limited and their work- 
men, which was received by the Central Government on tho 
3rd October, 1979.

SIONER (CENTRAL) HAZARIBAGH.

prises, At & P. O. Dugda, Dist. Giridih.

STATE BIHAR. 

Arbitration Award U/s, lOA of the I.D, Act 1947 


The Govt, of India, Ministry of Labour by their Order 
No. 1^20013'/! /79-D-ni( A) dated 3-7-79 published in the 
Gazette of India referred the following Indudrliil Dispute to me 
for arbitration under Sec, lOA of the 1. D. Act, 1947. 

"Whether the demand of the workmen that the nature of 
work of the workmen encaged in Excavation of 
Slurry inside ponds as well as outside ponds being 
identical, the workmen engaged in Excavation of 
slurry from outside ponds arc entitled to same rates 
of wag^ as payable to the workmen engaged for 
excavation of sluray inside ponds? If so, to what relief 
the workmen engaged in outside pond« are entitled?’’. 


The discussions on the above matter were held on 8-8-79 
and 6-9-79. 


2. The contention of the workmen is that the nature of 
work for excavation of sluriw from the inside ponds as well 
as outside ponds of Dugda Coal Washery of Steel Authority 
of India Ltd. P. O. Dugda Dist, Giridih are similar. Rather, 
the nature of work of the workmen engaged in excavation of 


slurry from outside ponds are more arduous and difficult than 
the work of e.xcuvation of slurry from inside ponds as the dis- 
tance of places where the slurry has to be placed after excava- 
tion is more in the outside pond in comparison to the distance 
covered by the workmen employed for Excav.ation of slurry in- 
side ponds. But the workmen engaged for excavation of slurry 
inside ponds arc paid at the rate of Rs. 5.10 per tonne whereas 
the workmen engaged for excavation of slurry from outside 
ponds are paid only at the rate of Rs. 4.10 per tonne. In 
view of the above the workmen ingaged for excavation of 
slurry from outside ponds should be paid the same wages as 
paid to the workmen engaged for excavation of slurry in- 
side ponds. 


3. In reply to the above M/s Dugda Rural Commercial 
Enterprises (Contractor)* contended that the nature of job 




performed by the workmen engaged for excavation of slurry 
from inside the pond and outside the ponds of 
Dugda Coal Washcry are quite different and there- 
fore, the workmen of outside pond are not justified to 
demand the rates of wages as paid to the workmen inside pond. 
However, the Contractors could not specify as to how the 
nature of work of inside is different fronr that of outside. 

4. The contention of the workmen that the workmen of 
outside pond has to stack shiny after the excavation at a 
distance more than the same covered by the workmen of in- 
side pond while stacking the sjuny could not be rebutted by 
the Contractors. After bearing the parties and the examination 
of the entire affairs T have come to (he conclusion that the 
nature of were of excavation and stacking of slurry in the 
inside pond as well as outside pond of Dugda Coal Washery 
of Steel Authority of Tndia Ltd. are similar and the workmen 
engaged in inside txind as well as outside ponds havei to puts 
similar nature of skill and work load. 

5. For the excavation of .slurry inside as well as outside 
ponds two different contractors have been engaged by the 
management of Dngda CoaT Washery of Steel Authority of 
India Ttd, While M /s Madho Singh have been engaged for 
excavation of Nlurry from the inside shiny pond M/s. Dugda 
Rural Commercial Enterprises Iiave been engaged for excava- 
tion of slimy from the oiitide slurry pond. As per the concili- 
ation settlement before me on 20-4-79 the workmen employed 
by M/.s. Madho Singh for Excavation of slurry from inside 
pond arc paid wages at the following rates : — 

(1) Excavation of slurry from the inside slurry pond and 
stacking the same at the bank of pond Rs, 5.10 
per tonne. 

(ii) Loading of the above shiny into Tipping Trucks/ 

Dumpers for dumping near the belt conveyor. 
— Rs. 2.55 per tonne. 

(iii) Loading of the dumped slurry brought by dampers 

on the running Conveyor belt — Rs. 2.55 per tonne. 

But the workmen employed by M/.s, Dugda Runxl Com- 
mercial Enterprises engaged for excavation of slurry from 
outside ponds are at present paid the wages at the following 
rates. — 

fa) Excavation of slurry from the outside .shiny pond and 
stacking the same at the bank of the pond at the 
rate of Rs. 4,10 paise per tonne, 

tb) T.oading of the above slurry into Trucks /Dumpers for 
dumping near the belt Conveyor — Rs. 2.55 per tonne. 

(C) Loading of the dumped slurry brought bv the Diipm- 
pers on the running Conveyor belt — R'. 2.55 per 
tonne, 

6. From the above it will be .seen that the wages for load- 
ing of the slurry into Trucks/Dumpers for Dumping near belt 
Conveyors and the loading of Ihe dumped shiny brought hv 
the dumpers on the running Conveyor belt are at the rate 
Rs. 2.55 paise tier tonne in rcapect of Inside and 
Outside Ponds but the rate of excavation of shirrv 
from in.side pond and outside pond are different. While 
workmen engaged for excavation of slurry from the 
inside pond and stacking the same on Ihe bank are 
paid at the rate Rs. 5.10 pai.sc per tonne from 20-4-79. the 
workmen employed for excavation of shiny from outside pond 
and stacking the same on the bank of the pond arc paid only 
at the rate Rs. 4.10 paise per tonne. I have aheady mentioned 
that the nature of work and the strain put by the workmen 
engaged for excavation of sluirv from inside pond irs well as 
outside pond are the same and therefore the workmen engaged 
for excavation of slurry from outside pond are fully justified 
to demand payment of wages at the same rate as paid to the 
workmen employed for Inside Pond, T, therefore, award (hat the 
workmen employed by M /s. Dugda Rural Commercial 
Enterprises for excavation of shirrv from outside pond should 
also be paid wages at the rate Rs. 5.10 per tonne w.e.f. 
20-4-79 as paid to the workmen employed for excavation of 
slurry from inside pond. 

S. B. STNOH, Asstt. T.aboiir Commissioner tCentral) 

Hazaribach Arbitrator

(Oorgaum Printing Prc.ss), Oorgaum Post. Kolar Gold Fields 
from the operation of the said Act for the period from the 
1st July, 1978 upto and inclusive of 30th June, 1979. 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following condi- 
tions, namely : — 

(1) The employer of the said factory shall submit in respect 
of the period during which that factory was subject to the 
operation of the said Act (hereinafter referred to as the said

lars a-s were due from it in respect of the said period under 
the Employees’ State Insurance (General) Regulations, 1950; 

(2) Any Inspector appointed by the Corporation under sub-

the Corporation authorised in this behalf shall, for the pur-

mitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 for the 
said period ; or

maintained as required by the Employees’ State 
Insurance (General) Regulation', I95(> for the said 
period ; or 

(iii) ascertaining whether the employees continue to be 
entitled to benefits provided by the employer in cash 
^nd kind being benefits in coiisideiation of which 
exemption Is being granted under this notification ; or 

(iv) ascertaining whether any of the provisions of the Act 

has been complied with during the period when such 
provisions were in foice In relation to the said 
factory ; be empowered to —

consider necessary ; or

agent or seiwant, or any person found in such faclorx',

account book or other document maintained in such 
factory, establishment, office or other premises,

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; OCTOBER 27, 1979/KARTIKA 5, 1901 


[Part IT— Sec. 3(ii)l 


Now Delhi, the 16th October, 1979 

S.O. 3639.~In pursuance of Hection 17 of the Induatrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 04 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby pubishes the following award of the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal, New Delhi in the industrial dis- 
pute between the employers in relation to the management 
of the Bcas Sutlej link Project, Sundeinagar and their work- 
man, which was received by the Central Govcinment on the 
I2th October, 1979. 

BEFORE SIIRT MAHESII CHANDRA, PRESDING OFFI- 
CER CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRTBU- 
NAT.-CUM-LABOUR COURT, NEW DELHI 

I. D. No. 9 of 1978 

In Re : 

Shrl Rattan Chand, Qr, No. 681-F.E, 

Nangal Township (Distt. Roopnagar) Petitioner 

VERSUS 

The Chief Engineer, 

Beas Satlej Link Project, 

Sundernagnr Township. 

PRESENT: 

Shri Rattan Chand with Shri S. P. Shah. 

Shri O. P. Gupta for the Manapement. 

AWARD; 

The Central Govt, as appropriate Govt, vide its order 
No. L-42012(3C)!/77-D.rrfB) dated the 26th December, 1977 
referred an Induatrial DisOTte u/s 10 of the 1. D, Act in 
the following terms to this Tribunal : 

‘Whether the section of the management of the Bcas 
Sutlej Link Project (Commuunication Division) in 
treating Shri Rattan Chand, Px-Greaser (Token No. 
481-Q) as having volunlarily resigned from service 
w.e.f. 12-11-1976 is justified ? If not, to what relief 
is the said workmen entitled ? 

2. On receipt of the reference usual notices were sent to 
the parties whereupon a statement of claim was filed on behalf 
of the workmen. Thereafter the Management filed a written 
statement. Finally a replication was filed by the workman. 
Upon the pleadings of the parties only issue framed was the 
one as referred to by the appropriate Govt, in the order of 
reference. Thereafter evidence of the parties was recorded 
and arguments were heard. T have gone through the evidence 
produced by the parties and after giving my considered 
thought to the matter before me I have come to the following 
findings ; 

3. Die contention of the workman is that he was appointed 
as a Greased on 22nd March, 1970 and was deputed to work as 
such under Executive Engineer, (Communication Division), 
Beds Sutlej link Project, Sunder Nagar under Token No. 
481<) and was governed by the Stajiding Orders of the 
Project certified under Industrial Employment Standing Orders 
Act, 1946. It is further stated by him that on 7/8th November. 

1976 he had proceeded on earned leave which w.as sanctioned 
upto 11-11-1978 but during this pcriotl he fell sick and could 
not resume his duty on 12-11-1976 and as such he applied 
for 20 days leave w.e.f. 12-11-1976 to 1-12-1976 on medical 
grounds atid thereafter another application for extension of 
leave by another 15 days for the period 2-12-1976 to 16-12-76 
was submitted and flutilly another application for leave from 
17-12-76 to 31-12-76 was submitted. All these applications 
were supported by medical certificates. The contention of the 
workman is that when he reported for duty on 1st January, 

1977 ho was not allowed to join duly and rather he was 
mfbrmed that a letter was being issued to him and he did 
receive such a letter No, 15/16 Wo dated 3-1-1977 whereby 
the leave applications were returned to him with the remark 
that ho had exceeded leave without pay beyond 90 days upto 
12-11-76 and thereafter he could not be granted any leave 
without a rncdical certificate of special disease referred to 
in the standing orders and as such he was considered absent 
from duty and whereupon he submitted an application dated 
8-1-77 to the Executive Engineer and yet nnotfier applic.ation 
on 9-2-77 requesting them to admit him on duty. Rather then 
giving duty to the workman he was informed vide letter no. 


3866-70 /761/15-E dated 13-2-77 that as his total leave without 
pay have come to 141 days during the calendar year 1976 he 
was deemed to have voluntarily resigned from 12-11-76. There 
after this reference was made. 

4. The facts as stated by the workman arc not by and 
large denied by the Management and rather it is urged on 
behalf of the Management that in accordance with the Stand- 
ing Orders leave beyond 90 days could not be sanctioned 
unless medical certificate from the Medical Officer of a civil 
hospital was submitted by the workmtm which was not done 
by him and as such he was treated to have resigned in accor- 
dance with standing orders due to over stayal of sanctioned 
leave beyond 11-11-76. 

5. In order to support his contention the workman has come 
forward as W.W. 1 and reiterated what was state^l in his 
application and has also tendered Ex, W/1 to Ex. W/9, copies 
of various letters into his evidence. During cross examination 
it is admitted by the workman that his Head Quarters was 
Nangal. It is also admitted by him that he was suffering from 
high blood pressure and during cross examination he has 
tendened Ex. W/1 to Ex. W/14. 

6. The Management in rebuttal ha,9 examined Shri Gurbux 
Singh, SDO as M. W. 1 who ha.s .stated that the services of 
Rattan Chand were tenninated w.e.f. 13-11-76 as having been 
voluntarily resigned. If is also staled by him that the work- 
man had proceeded on three days leave w.e.f. 8-11-76 to 
11-11-76 but he did not report for duty after the expiry of 
this leave. It is further stated by him that his leave application 
was received on 12-12-76 for the period 12-11-76 to 30-11-76 
and that no application for leave was received before 1-12-76 
for this jperioc. It is also stated that no application for leave 
was received for subsequent perioti except on 16-12-76 for 
the period 2-12-76 to 16-12-76 and that these applications 
were not granted as he had already availed his 50 days leave 
without pay. He has produced a copy of the certified stand- 
mg orders which is Ex. M.W. 1/1, 


7. It is in the light of this evidence that the contention 
of the workman has to be weighed, Thi.s is correct that the 
absence beyond 11-11-76 till 1-1-77 was unanthorised, more 
80 because no leave application was submitted until the leave 
had been enjoyed by the workman. Each of the applications 
are stated to have been r^eived by the office according to 
M.W.l after the expiiy of the i'especfi\’e period of leave. 
Further more the leave was not sanctioned either and rather 
leave was refused and ultimately the leave applications were 
returned and the termination of the sciwices of this workman 
is the rcsidt of coming into operation o.<^ the certified standing 
orders. According to the Standing Orders it was essential 
for the workman to submit a medical certificate from Civil 
Surgeon, Nangal wh'xh wa.s his Head Qu.irtcrs but the workman 
did not care to submit any medical certificate from the Civil 
Surgeon, Nangal rather from the perusal of his applications on 
record I find that every time he applied for leave supported 
by a medical certificate purported to have Iwn issued by an 
ordinary Reffistered Medical Pr.TCtitioner. According to the 
provisions of para KXc) of the Standing Orders Ex, M.W.l /I 
it was essential for the workman to submit a medical certi- 
ficate from the Principal Medical Officer of the Project or 
authorised Medical Officer of the Institute which he failed to 
do. But the grant of leave without pay beyond 90 days 
could be qnly for "pecified four categories of diseases 
meutioned in sub para 3 of para lOfcl of Ihe Standing Orders 
and these diseases are Piilmonarv Tuberculosis, Tuberanlosis 
of any other part, Leprosy or Mental illness. Admittedly this 
workman was not suffering from any of these disca,se.s. A 
perusal of the various medical certificates .attached with the 
application for leave shows that (he workman was running 
high Wood pres.siirc only which according to Ihc Certified 
Standing Orders did not admit of grant of e.xlra ordinary 
leave beyond 90 days in any case. If is in these circumstances 
that the representations of the workman filed on 8th and 9th 
January were also not allowed and rather it was decided that 
the workman shall be deemed to have resigned w.e.f. 11-11-76. 
Para n,o.l3 and 14 of the Standing Orders deal with over 
stayal of leave and absence with out in so far as the leave 
applied was not granted and could not be granted. The work- 
man had evidently over stayed his leave granted till 11-11-1976 
and he shall bo deemed to be absent without leave from 
till 1-1-77 and hence in accordance with para 14(b) 
of the Certified Standing Orders he will be deemed to have 



[wTiT TT — ;i(ii)] 


3029 


^THTT JfiT 27, 1979/^Tr?rT 5, 1901 


resigned from the service of the Project of his own accord.’ 
The workman hag not been able to show that there was 
any malafidc on the part of the Management in treating him 
abwnt from duty oi' in treating him to have abandoned his 
service and thereby voluntarily resigned within the meaning 
of ^ara 14 of the Certified Standing Orders. It is no where 
demed by the workman that he had not actually enjoyed 90 
days leave without pay uptil 11-11-76. The statement on oath 
of M.W.l stand unrebutted and must be accepted as correct. 
The existence of a civil hospital at Nangal which the place 
of posting of workman has also not been denied by the work- 
man and inspitte thereof no explanation was made by the 
workman at any stage before the Management or this Tribunal 
to explain why he could not obtain medical certificate from 
the Tnchage of Civil Hospital. It is urged on behalf of the 
workman that the Management should have taken disciplinary 
action against the workman for unauthorised absence but it 
is the discretion of the Management and if the Standing Orders 
permit the Management to treat the workman to have 
realgned from the service it was open to the Management to 
have re-course to such Standing Orders. Ex. W/1, \V1/3 and 
W/4 are the copies of various leave applications while Ex. 
^^l/2 is the copy of medical certiJicatc, Ex. W/5 is the letter 
dated 3-1-1977 whereby the applications of the workman were 
returned with the remark that he had exceeded leave with- 
out pay beyond 90 days upto 12-11-76 and it also called 
upon the workman to send his reply to the said letter .and 
if the reply was not received within three days it was notified 
to the workman by this letter that he will he considered as 
voluntarily resigned from service. Ex. W/6 is the letter sent 
by the workman in pursuence thereof and Ex. W/7 is the 
letter dated 9-2-77. Ex. W/8 is the final order whereby the 
v/orkman was treated to have voluntarily reaigned from the 
service. Later on the orginals were also tiled by the workman 
and the same are on record. Ex. W/IO to Ex. W/12 are the 
applications and Ex. W/13 and Ex. W,/I4 are the medical 
certificates. From my discussion above, I find that the services 
of the workman have been treatea to have came to an end 
in consequence of his having absented from the duty without 
any permiission and he has been treated to have lesigned 
from the job in accordance with the standing orders. He roala- 
fidc on the part of the Management has been proved. In view 
thereof T have come to the conclusion that the reference 
should be aiMwerd in the affirmative and accordingly I hold 
that the action of the Management of Beas Sutlej Link 
Project fCommunicatlon Division') in treating Shri Rattan 
Chand Ex. Greaser (Token No. 48I-Q) as having voluntarily 
resigned from the service w.e.f. 12-11-76 is justified and that 
the workman is not entitled to any rcl'ef whatsoever. 1 do 
not think that the provisions of Section 25-P are even remotely 
attracted by the case of the workman in. as much as there 
is no clement of surplusage involved and the post on which 
this workman was working has not been proved to have been 
abolished. Parties would bear their own costs. 


FURTHER AWARDED ; 

That requisite number of copies of this award may be sent 
to the appropriate Govt, for necessary action at theiV end. 

MAHESH CHANDRA, Presiding Officer. 


Dated: the 12th September, 1979.

S.O, 3640. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Indus- 
trial' Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947). the Central Govern- 
ment hereby publishes the following award of the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal, New Delhi in the industrial 
dispute betwee the employers in relation to the manage- 
ment of Beas Sutlej Link Project and their workman, which 
was received by the Central Government on the 1 2tli Octo- 
ber, 1979. 


BEFORE SHRI MAHESH CHANDRA, PRESIDING OFFL 
CER, CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBU- 
NAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT, NEW DELHI. 

I, D. No. 19 of 1977 

In re ; 

Shri Zulfi Ram c/o Shri N. P. Sharma, General Secre- 
tary, B.S.L. Workers Union, H. No. 568. S/II, 
Sundernagor Township — -Petitioner. 

Versus 

The Chief Engineer, Beas Sutlej l.ink Project, Sunder- 
nagar Township. 

PRESENT ; 

Shri Ishwar Chander — for the workman. 

Shri O. P, Gupta,— for the Management. 

award 

The Central' Uovornment as appropriate Government vide 
Its order No. L-42012(17)/76-D. 11(B) dated the 23rd 

Fcbruaiy, 1977, as rcfcrretl an Induijtrial Dispute for adju- 
dication to (his Tribunal u/s 10 ol the I.D. Act, 1947 in the 
following terras : 

“Whether the management of Beas Sutlej Link Project 
is justified in treating Shri Zulfi Ram, Assistant 
Foreman Special as a fresh entrant to the establish- 
ment with effect from 17th September, 1975 and 
whether his retrenchment, thus with effect from 
3rd July, 1976 is justified '! If not, to what relief 
is the said workman entitled ?“ 

2. On receipt of the reference u.sual notices were sent to 
the parties, whereupon a statement of claim was filed by the 
workman and « written statement was filed by the Mana^- 
ment and an opportunity wa.s afforded to file the replication 
tO' the workman but no replication was filed. In consequence 
following one, preliminary issue was framed vide my order 
dated the 7fh January, 19'78 : 


1. Whether Shri Zulfi Ram is a workman within the 
meaning of I.D. Act, 1947 ? 

3. Thereafter evidence of the parties was recorded anJ the 
issue was set up for arguments but an application was moved 
on behalf of the Management for additional evidence which 
was allowed vide my order dated the 6th January, 1979 on 
payment of Rs, 100 as conditional costs and thereupon the 
only evidence parties led was that they produce certain docu- 
ments and Shri 0. P. Gupta, the representative of the 
Management and Shri Ishwar Chander, the representative of 
the workman came forward with the following statement on 
9th June, 1979 : 

“The documents produced by the parties on the file 
be read 'ft® evidence without formal proof and 
parties close additional evidence." 

4. I have gone through the evidence produced by the 
parties and have heard their representatives at length on this 
preliminary issue and after givmg my considered thought to 
the matter before me I have come to the following findings; 


5, The Management has in prelitninary objection to the 
written statement submitted that 'Shri Zulfi Rani was emp- 
loyed as an Assistant Foreman (Special) in the Beas Sutlej 
Link Project he was drawing salary more than Rs. 500 and 
was working in supervisory capacity. The Certified Standing 
Orders are applicable to all the employees of the Proj^L 
whether they arc workman under the I.D. Act or not Shri 
Zulfi Ram was not a workman as defined under the Indutt- 
trial Dispute Act and therefore this question may be decided 



3030 


[Part n — S ec, 3(ii)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 27, 1979/KARTIKA 5 , 1901 


before taking up the dispute. No repheation was tiled to 
this prelumnaiy objection by the workman inspite of oppor- 
tunity alforded in this behalf. The workman has appeared 
himself aa W.W. 1 and has admitted m his statement m 
cheif that he had joined service with Bhakra Dam on 16th 
October, 1950 and his services were later transferred to Beas 
Sutlej Lmk Project and on 17th September, 1975 he was 
working as AssisUmt Poreraan Specif. It is admitted by bim 
that ho was working as Assitant Foreman even on 3rd July. 
1976. It is stated by him that he was promoted as Assistant 
Foreman because of his previous experience. It is also ad- 
mitted by him that his basic was Ks. 450 at that time and 
he used to get a gross salary of Rs. 864 ^r month. During 
cross-examination it is admitted by him that Ex. R/1 is the 
correct copy of Standing Orders duly ccrtilled by the Chief 
Labour Commissioner, Government of india and that he was 
having about 10 to 15 persons in his gang. It is also ad- 
mitted by him that ho used to mark attendance of his gang 
peopre which was later checked by S.D.O. and Overseer. It 
is mso further admitted that he used to have Beldar, Driller 
and Blastman in his gang and that he used to see the work of 
fitters, blastmen and driller to lind out if they were doing the 
work correctly and if not he used to instruct them and guide 
them. 

6. Thus from his very admission as W.W. 1 it is apparent 
that Shri ZuLh Ram was working in supervisory capacity and 
he used to supervise the work of fitters, blastmen and charge- 
men and mark their attendance and guide them, It is also 
etablished that he was also drawing more than Rs. 860 per 
month. All these facts go to establish that certainly Shii 
Zulfi Ram was a supervisor drawing more than Rs. 500 per 
month as his salary and as such was not covered by the defi- 
nition of workman as given in the I.D, Act, 1947. This fact 
is further corroborated from the testimony of Shri Gokal 
Chand, SDO of the respondent who has been examined as 
M.W. 1 and he has stated that he was- SDO(Field) and knew 
Zulfi Ram since 1971 and that Zulfi Ram was Assistant Fore- 
man (Special) which was a supervisory category and Zulfi 
Ram was incharge of a gang and it was his duty to get work, 
from gangmen which varied from 10 tb 15 and sometimes 
more. He has also produced attendance cards of tire gang- 
men Ex MW. 1/1 to Ex. M. W. 1/3 and he has stated that 
those cards bear the sdgnaturcs of Zulfi Ram under various 
dates in token of having made entries as supervisor. It is 
further stated by him that Zulfi Ram dij not work with his 
own hands. Finally it is stated by him that there were two 
rates of payment of hazardous allowance, one for the work- 
man and the other for the supervisor .and Shri Ziilh Ram had 
always drawn the allowance as .supervior. During cross- 
examination he has denied that Zulfi Ram was doing work 
with his own hands or that he used to do may drilling or 
blasting work himself. The statement of this witness also 
goes to show that certainly Zulfi Ram was a supervisor draw^ 
ing more than Rs. 500 per month as his salary and as such 
was not a workman under the I.D. Act, 1947. 

7. My attention has been drawn on behalf of the Manage- 
ment to a copy of letter No. 1194-1234/123-SL dated 29th 
August, 1973 from the perusal whereof I find that the Asstt. 
Foreman (Special) was categorised as a supervisor staff 
and finds place at item No. ^ of the list attached to the copy 
of the said letter. 

8. In view of my discussions above, 1 have come to the 
conclusion that Shri Zulfi Ram was a supervisor drawing 
more than Rs. 500 per month as his salary and as such could 
not be deemed to be a workman under the I.D. Act, 1947 
and in view thereof it would follow that his case cannot be 
covered by the I.D. Act and as such this reference is not 
maintainble and as such it is awarded that Zulfi Ram not 
being a workman this reference is not maintainable and he 
cannot be granted any relief in this reference. Parties in the 
peculiar circumstances would bear their own costa. 


Further Awarded ; 

The requisite number of copies of this award may be sent 
to the appropriate Govt, for necessary action at their end. 

MAHESH CHANDRA, Presiding Officer. 

Dated : 10-9-1979. 

19th Bhadra, 1901.

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; OCTOBER 27, 1979/KARTlKA 5, 1901 

MINISTRY OF LABOUR 
New Delhi, the 27 April, 1978 

S.O 3642,— Ta pursuance of section 36 of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Financial Estimates and 
Performance Budget 1978-79 of the Employees’ State Insurance Corporation, as finally adopled by the said Corporation, are hereby 
published for general information. 

EMPLOYEES’ STATE INSURANCE CORPORATION 

Ex.planatory Memorandum on the Revised Estimates for the year 1977-78 & Budget Estimates for the year 1978-79 

The Budget Estimates of the Receipts and Expenditure of the Employees’ State Insurance Corporation for the financial year 
1977-78 were approved by the Standing Commiltce and the Corporation at the meetings held on the 22nd & 23rd February, 1977 
respectively. These were approved by the Central Government in the Ministry of Labour Icetter No. G. 20017/2/77 — HI dated the 
31st March, 1977. 

2. The Budget Estimates approved by the Central Government covered — 

(i) provisions needed for the running of the Scheme in various Centres where it has already been implemented; and 

(ii) funds needed for the extension of the scheme to new areas. 

3. At the time of preparing the Budget Estimates for 1977-78, it was anticipated that (i) the Scheme would be extended to new areas 
and (ii) medical care would be extended to the families of the Insured Persons as per programme detailed in Appcndix-1 from the dates 
shown against each in columns 3 & 5 thereof. However, due to administrative and other difficulties in making adequate medical arrange- 
ments by the State Governments concerned, the programme of implementation and extension of medical care to the families had to be 
modified. The Scheme was actually extended to some of the areas from the later dates as .shown in column 4 of Appcndix-I. As regards 
the areas where the expectation to extend the Scheme could not materialise, the revised dates, as now anticipated, have been indicated 
ag iinst each item in appropriate columns of the above referred Appendix. 

4. As a result of further correspondence with the varioas State Governments, it is now anticipa ted that the Scheme will be implemented 
in new areas, including new Sectors of employment, during the financial years 1977-78 and 1978-79 from thedatc.s as shown in Appendix-11. 

■■ For the sake of convenience, the places where the Scheme has already been implemented in the past with dates of implementation, have 
also been indicated in this Appendix. The number of employees already covered or proposed to be covered has also been revised in the 
light of latest information vailable and incorporated in Appenoix-ll. 

5. The Revised Estimates for the financial year 1977-78 and Budget Estimates for the year 1978-79 have been prepared in the light 
of the revised programme of implementation. Further, the budget estimates have been prepared in the revised form approved by the Stand- 
ing Committee and the Corporation in October, 1977, and by the Central Government in November, 1977.

Revised Estimates for 1977-78 and Budget Estimates for 1978-79 are submitted for consideration and approval.

Brief explanations for some of the important items under the various heads 

are furnished in 

the following paragraphs. 



7. Contributions 


Employers’ and Employees’ shares of contribution are payable through a single contribution stamp as per rates in Schcdulc-I of the 
ESI Act, 1943 as modified by the ESI Amendment Act, 1975. 

8. Medical Benefits 

8.1, The expenditure under the head "A- Medical Benefits except for the Union Territory of Delhi where the Scheme is directly 
administered by the Corporation, is initially incurred by the State Governments and is later shared between the Corporation and .he 
State Governments in the prescribed ratio of 7 ; 1, The maximum shareable amount is subject to the ceilings fixed by the Corporation 
from time to time. The provision made under this head is intended to cover the Corporation’s share of the expenditure, 

8,2, Coiling on E,xponditurc an Medical Benefils 


The ceilings of yearly shareable expenditure on medical benefits per employee have been revised as follows from 1st April, 1977. 


Type of Medical care 

Old ceiling Revised 

Restricted 

ceiling 

Rs.70 Rs. 70 

Expanded 

Rs. 75 Rs.80 

Full 

Rs.9S Rs. 105 

Payment to State Governments 

The Corporation makes during the year ‘On Account’ payments Upto 90% of its share of expenditure on 

medical benefits, on the 


basis of expenditure statements received from the State Governments, subject to adjustments on receipts of audit certificates from the 
Concerned State Accountant General. 

8,4. Expenses Incufred directly by the Corporation 

The provision made under the head “Medical treatment and care and maternity facilities (expenses incurred directly by the Corpo- 
ration)” includes the estimated cost of administration of the Medical care to the Insured Persons and their families in the Union 
Territory of Delhi. The anticipated recovery at the rate of 1 /8th of shareable amount has been taken into account in the Revised 
Estimates 1977-78 and Budget Estimates 1978-79 on the Revenue side under the head “Stale Oovemmcnl 's/Union Territories” share 
towards medical benefit initially incurred by the Corporation, 

REVISED ESTIMATES FOR THE YEAR 1977-78 
1— RECEIPTS 


9,1 . The Revenue of the Corporation for the current year 1977-78 is now estimated at Rs. 1 ,40,00 . 00 lakhs as against Rs. 1,56,63,81 
lakhs assumed in the Budget. 


CONTRIBUTIONS 


9,2. The income from Contributions is now anticipated at Rs. 1,27,36.34 lakhs against Rs. 1,44,96.28 lakhs at the Budget 
stage. The downward revision of Budget Estimates for 1977-78 has been necessitated mainly because of the following factors. 

(1) At the time of preparation of Budget Estimates 1977-78 it was anticipated that 59.30 lakhs employees (weighted average during 
the year) would be covered under the Scheme. But the expectations have not materialised due to administrative and other diflficultics 
of the State Governments who were to implement the Scheme. The total number of covered employees as on 31-10-1977 was 55.26 
hkhs. In respect of further coverage during the year 1977-78, the receipts from contributions during the year will not be substantial. 
The Revise.I Estimates have, therefore, been framed on the basis of receipts during the first eight months of the current financial year 
an! proportionate anticipated receipts during the remaining four months. The shortfall on this account is about Rs. 9.85,78 lakhs. 

(ii) The avarage pet capita income from contributions during the year 1975-76 was Rs. 161.94, However, during the first six months 
of 1976-77 it rose to Rs. 243 .18 mainly on account of amendment of the E.S.l. Act as a result of which employees drawing over 
Rs.SOO. per month and upto Rs, 1000 per month came under the Scheme with effect from 30th Novem'jer, 1975. Consequently a slightly 
higher rate of Rs. 244.46 was adopted for the Budget Estimates 1977-78. The average per capita contrbiution during the first six months 
of 1977-78 has, however, comedown to Rs, 230.79. The fall in the per capita contribution rate appears to be due to the fact that the sub- 
sequent additional coverage has mainly been in the smaller sectors of employment bringing in predominantly such workers under the 
Scheme as are in the lower wage groups. The shortfall on lhi.s accounts amounts to about Rs. 7,46. 01 lakhs.

lise the arrears.

Budget Estimates of Rs. 7,90.67 lakhs, 

9.5. RENT OF HOSPITAL AND DISPENSARY BUILDINGS OWNED BY THE CORPORATION 

The income of the Corporation from rent of ESI Hospital and dispensary buildings together with the residential stafl' quarters attach- 
ed thereto is anticipated to be Rs. 284,10 lakhs. Tne rent so recovered will form a p.trt of the shareable expenditure Incurred by the 
State Governments on the provision of medical benefits to the Insured Persons and would, thus, be automatically apportioned between 
the Corporation and the State Governments in the proscribed ratio of 7 ; 1 . 

II— EXPENDITURE 

10, The expenditure on Revenue Account in the current year 1977-78 is now estimated to be Rs. 1,16,77.17 lakhs as against 
Rs. 1,22,39.52 lakhs anticipated in the Budget, 

BENEFITS TO INSURED PERSONS & THEIR FAMILIES 
A— MEDTCAl BENEFITS 

11.1. The total provision under this head is Rs. 47,12. 38(a) lakhs which comprises Rs. 44,49.26 lakhs as Corporation's share of 
expenditure on medical treatment and equipment for hospitals ; Rs. 252.62 lakhs as expenditure on Medical Benefits in Delhi where the 
Scheme is directly administered by the Corporation and Rs. 10.50 lakhs towards the payment of confinement Tees payable unlder the 
Scheme to the beneficiaries in Maharashtra Region. In respect of Delhi, the recovery of 1 /8lh expenditure has been taken into account 
on the receipt side of the Budget in 1 978-79. The 1 /8th share of confinement charges due from Maharashtra State will be adjusted while 
reimbursing their claim for expenditure on medical benefits. 

(a) Includes arrear payments (Rs. 4,19.13 lakhs) for earlier years. 

The Increase In the revised estimates in the expenditure on medical benefits is partly on account of post-budget enhancement of the 
ceilings for medical care referred in paragraph 8.2 above. 

B-CASH BENEFITS 

1 1 .2. The duration of sickness benefit payable under the Employees* State Insurance Act has been enhanced with effect from 1 .5,1977 
from 56 days to 91 days in any two consecutive benefit periods bringing It at par with the minimum standards laid down by the I.L.O 
for developing countries. 

The Permanent Disablement Benefit and Dependants, Benefit pensions payble in cases of insured persons who suffered disablement 
or death on or before 31-3-1975 have also been increased from 1st October, 1977, to the following extent, on account of the increase in 
the cost of living index. 

(a) Cases where disablement or death occurred on or before 31-3-1974 20 % of the amount subject to the rounding off to 

the next higher multiple of 5 paise. 

(b) Cases whore disablement or death occutred between 1-4-74 & 31-3-75. JO % of the amoimt subject to the rounding off to 

the next higher multiple of 5 paise. 

11.3. provision of Rs. 39,80,22 lakhs made for the various Cash Benefits is based on the progress of actuals for the first eight months 
of the financial year 1977-78 and the anticipated requirement for the remaining four months. It takes into account the adjustments detail- 
ed in paragraph 11.4 below. 

adjustments in PERMANENT DISABLEMENT BENEFIT & DEPENDANTS’ BENEFIT RESERVE FUNDS 

11.4. It has been assessed that during 1977-78 a provision of Rs, 7,47, 15 lakhs is required towards capitalised value of Permanent 
Disablement Benefit. Similarly, a provision of Rs. 2,49.22 lakhs is required towards capitalised value of Dependants, Benefit. These 
provisions include Rs. 1,76.00 lakhs and Rs. 1,04.00 lakhs to be transferred to the P.D.B, and D.B, Reserve Funds, respectively, as one 
time adjustment cost (in the form of capitalised value) on account of increase in rates w.e.f. 1-10-1977. 

However, the Valuer has in the fifth Quinquennial Valuation of the Corporation as on 31st March, 1974, determined surpluses 
of Rs. 2,72 . 75 lakhs and Rs. 8 . 26 lakhs In the P.D.B. and D.B. Reserve Funds, respectively. These figures have been up-dated and based 
on actuarial calculations it has been determined that as on 3 (-3-1978 there would be a surplus of Rs. 3,48.24 lakhs in the P.DB, Reserve 
Fund and Rs, 62. 34 lakhs in the D,B. Reserve Fund. In view of these surpluses, net provision of Rs. 1,98.91 lakhs has been made in the 
P.D.B. Reserve Fund and Rs. 1,86.88 lakhs in the D.B, Reserve Fund in the Revised Estimates 1977-78 referred in paragraph 11.3 above. 

C— OTHER BENEFITS 

12. A provision of Rs. 12.37 lakhs has been made in the Revised Estimates, against Budget Estimates of Rs. 12.10 lakhs under 
C-Other Benefits, to cover expanses on miscallaneous bsneflts e.g. cost of artificial limbs to the amputated insured persons, charges rela- 
ting to Medical Boards and payment to insured persons on account of conveyance and or loss of wages etc,

Rs. 10,72 . 80 lakhs anticipated in the budget estimates. The provision is based on the actual expenditure incurred during the first eight mont- 
hs of the year 1977-73 (including actuals of pay & allowances for nine months) and the expenditure likely to be incuned during the

TOTAL 

SHORTFALL IN PENSION RESERVE FUND 

13.3. The Valuer appointed by the Government of India in his Fifth Quinquennial Valuation of the E.S.I.C. as on 31-3-1974 has 
pointed out a shortfall of Rs. 6.81 lakhs In the Pension Reserve Fund. According to actuarial calculations the shortfall as on 31-3-1978 
works out to Rs. 8.60 lakhs. The provision of Rs. 64.70 lakhs for Pension Reserve Fund in the Revised Estimates includes the above 
sum. 

CHANGE IN ACCOUNTING OF INTEREST ON INVESTMENT OF PROVIDENT FUND BALANCES 

13.4. In the earlier years the interest earned on investments of Provident Fund was reflected as reduction in expenditure on pay- 
ment of interest to subscribers. It has been considered more appropriate to include the receipts on account of interest on Provident Fund 
investments as receipts. The interest paid to subscribers, therefore, now appears as a distinct item of expenditure. 

HOSPITALS/DISPENSARIES 

14. The provision under this head comprises (i) Depreciation of Hospital/Dispensary buildings (Rs. 31,54 lakhs) and (ii) Repair 
and Maintenance of these buildings (Rs. 91.00 lakhs) as per the percentages of capital cost fixed for the purpose i. e, 1 % and 2.9% res- 
pectively. 

CONTRIBUTIONS TO CAPITAL CONSTRUCTION & EMERGENCY RESERVE FUNDS 
CAPITAL CONSTRUCTION RESERVE FUND 

15.1. As in the previous years, 10% of the total revenue derived from Employers’ and Employees’ Contribution is to be credited 
to the Capital Construction Reserve Fund for construction of hospitals/dispensaries/other Medical institutions and office buildings/stafl' 
quarters in the ratio of 8 ; 2, respectively. 

Accordingly provision of Rs. 12,73 .63 lakhs has been made in the Revi.scd Estimates for 1977-78. 

EMERGENCY RESERVE FUND 

15.2. As decided by the Corporation in its meeting held on 17th March, 1973, 20% of the excess of income over expenditure, subject 
to a minimum of Rupees One crore and whole of the excess when it is less than one crore, is to be credited to the "Emergency Reserve 
Fund" accordingly, provision of Rs. 5,80.71 lakhs has been made in the Revised Estimates for 1977-78. 

EXPENDITURE ON CAPITAL ACCOUNT 

16. The amount originally provided for expenditure on Capital Account for construction wo'ks was Rs, 13,48.00 lakhs cempi i- 
sing (i) Rs. 1 .48 lakhs for the construction of office buildings (including staff quarters) & (ii) Rs. 12,00.00 lakhs for the construction 
of hospitals and dispensaries. 

A provision of Rs . 0 . 60 lakh was made for the purchase of staff cars. 

The provision for Rs. 9,22.47 lakhs has been made in the Revised Estimates 1977-78 as follows:— 

(a) OFFICE BUILDINGS (INCLUDING STAFF QUARTERS) 

The provision of Rs. 1,48 ,00 lakhs made in the Budget Estimates for 1977-78 has been reduced to Rs. 81 .02 lakhs in the Revised Esti- 
mates for 1977-78. This is based on the trend of actuals and anticipated payments. 

(b) BUILDINGS OF HOSPITALS AND DISPENSARIES 

The Provision of Rs. 12,00.00 lakhs under this head has also been reduced to Rs. 8,41 .45 lakhs in the Revised Estimates for 1977- 
78 on the basis of trend of actuals and anticipated payments.

BUDGET estimates FOR THE YEAR 1978-79 
I— RECEIPTS 
CONTRIBUTION 

18.1 Income on account of contributions (Employers and Employees’ Shares) has been estimated at Rs. 1,33 .35,78 lakhs bearing 
in mind (a) the Revised Estimates for 1977-78, (b) expected number of 58.02 lakhs ’covered’ employees (weighted average) during 1978- 
79 and (c) the anticipated per capita annual income of about Rs. 230 from contributions. 

INTEREST FROM INVESTMENT OF SURPLUS CASH BALANCES 

18.2. Receipts on account of Interest from the investments of surplus cash balance arc expected to be Rs. 7,51 .46 lakhs as against 
the Revised Estimates of R.s, 7,94.02 lakhs, Sincethe investments are now being made in ’Rc-investment Plan' of the State Tank of India 
inder which interest falling due will be credited to the Corporation’s account only after 61 months from the date of ivestment, whereas 
the Investments made earlier to the introduction of Re-investment Plan’ brought interes monthly to the accounts of the Corporation, 
there is a decrease in receipts, 

rent of hospital AND DISPENSARY BUILDINGS OWNED BY THE CORPORATION 

18.3, A sum of Rs. 2,84. 10 lakhs is expected to be recovered from the State Governments on account of rent of the Hospital 
and Dispensary buildings owned by the Corporation. 

II— EXPENDITURE 

19. The increased provision under the various heads in the Budget Estimates tor the year 1978-79 as compared to corresponding 
provision in the Revised Estimates fi r the year 1977-78 is mainly duo to — 

(i) the improvement in type of medical care to the farnilies of Insured Persofis; 

(ii) the extension of the Scheme to new arcas/ostablishnionts; 

(iii) the operation of the Scheme for full year in areas, including establishments, where the implementation has been brought during 
the year 1977-78; and 

(iv) expected increase in employment and wages in the implemented areas, 

A— Medical Benefits 

20.1- A total provision of Rs, 55,35.08 lakhs, including on amount of Rs. 9,56.34 lakhs for past liabilities, has been made in the 
Budget Estimates 1978-79 for medical benefits in the light of Revised Estimates for 1977-78 and anticipated additional coverage during the 
year. The number of ‘covered’ employees during 1978-79 has been estimated at 38,02 lakhs (weighted average). The provision includes 
Rs. 2,79 ,04 lakhs to be incurred directly by the Corporation during 1978-79 for providing medical care to the Insured Persons and their 
families in the Union Territory of Delhi and also Rs. 10. 50 lakhs to be spent directly by the Corporation towards payment of confinement 
fees in the State of Maharashtra. l/8th of the latter Re. 10,50 lakhs will be deducted from the claim of the State Government for the 
reimbursenient of expenses incurred by it on the provision of medical benefits to the Insured Persons in the State. 

The average approximate cost of medical care per employee per annum as provided in the Budget Estimates based on amount due

Introduction of New Benefit — -Invalidity Benefit 

22. The Corporation at its meeting held on 25th October, 1977, approved the Scheme of Invalidity Benefit to be introduced in the 
Employees’ State Insurance Act, to cover the contingency ol invalidity due to causes other than employment injury. As the Scheme requrircs 
amendment of the Employees State Insurance Act, it has been sent to the Central Government for consideration and consequential amend- 
ment to the Act. 

The consideration and approval of the Scheme by the Central Government and amondmet of the Act will take time, No budget pro- 
vision has, therefore, been made for Invalidity Benefit cither in the Revised Estimates 1977-78 or in the Budget Estimates 1978-79. It will 
be possible to meet this entire expenditure on Invalidity Benefit within the existing contribution income. In case the amendment to the Act 
is made during the year 1978-79, necessary sanction for additional budget provision to meet the expenditure on Invalidity Benefit will 
be solicited from the Corporation at the apprpriate time. 

ADMINISTRATION EXPENSES 

23.1. The Administration expenses have been exhibited under two heads, viz. “(A)— Superintendence” and "(B) — Field-Work". 

23.2. Provision on account of pay and allowances has been made for posts which have already been sanctioned as also for posts 
required on further extension and for certain new Centres etc. A statement showing the details of the staff of the Corporation as it stood 
sanctioned on 1-4-1977 is at Appendix-Ill. The increase which is due to progressive expansion of the Employees State Insurance Scheme 
is as per norms approved by the Central Government. 

23.3. A statement showing details of provision made under the head ‘Allowances and Honoraria’ is at Appendix-lV. 

CONTINGENCIES (BOTH UNDER A--SUPERINTENDENCE AND B- FIELD WORK) AND C— OTHER CHARGES 

23.4. The provision under the various sub-heads which are self explanatory has been made mainly on the basis of actuals for the 
first 8 months of the year 1977-78 and anticipated requirements for further extension of the Scheme. 

EXPENSES ON ADMINISTRATION PER EMPLO'l'EE PER ANNUM

Estimates 1978-79 will be Rs. 16.97 and Rs. 17.18 respectively, against the figure of Rs. 18 . 10 anticipated at the Budget .stage.
