PERFORMANCE BUpCiET OF THE EMPLOYEES’ STATE INSURANCE CORPORATION FOR THE YEAR 1978-79 
INTRODUCTORY : 

The Employees’ State Insurance Act 1948, is the only piece of Social insurance legislation in India which provides for henolits in con- 
tingencies of sickness, maternity and employment injury to certain categories of industrial workers. 

COVERAGE ; 

2, The Employees' State Insurance Act 1948, applies to all non-sensonal factories using power wherein 20 or moie peiior.s are en - 
) 1 oyed for wages. The Scheme is being implemented in a phased manner areawise. The Scheme is being further extended to now classes of 

establishments namely, power using factories employing 10-19 persons and non-power using factories, shops, Ihcrtics, cincmEs, hotels 
restaurants, motor transport undertakings and newspaper establishments employing 20 or more per.sons. 

PRINCIPAL objects OF THE SCHEME ; 

3. The Corporation provides the following benefits to the Insured Persons and their families. 

1 . Medical Benefit 

Medical Care is provided through the ESI Hospitals and Dispensaries- 

2. Cash Benefits 

(i) Sickness benefit when an insured person falls sick. 

(ii) Maternity benefit to female insured workers. 

(iii) Temporary/Permanont Disablement benefits to Insured Persons who meet with accident during the course of employment 

(iv) Dependants’ Benefit to the families of Insured Persons who die as a result of employment injury. 

(vj Funeral Benefit to the family members or the nearest relation of the deceased insured worker to perform his funeral rites. 
The quantum of the benefits is given in paragraph 12.2 below. 

administration ; 

4 The Employees’ State Insurance Scheme is administered by a corporate body called the Employees’ State Insurance CorporAtion-, 
hich has members representing employers, employees, the Central and Stale Governments, Medical profession and Parliament. The 
'^ro'vlsion of medical care under the Employees’ State Insurance Scheme is the responsibility of the State Governments except in Delhi 

where the Corporation itself arranges medical care. ^ ^ 



3096 


[Part H— Sec. 3(ii)] 


THE GA2;ETrE OF INDIA ; OCTOBER 27, 1979/KARTIKA 5. 1901 

FINANCE: " ^ ~ — - — 

5. The Employees’ State Insurance Scheme is mainly financed by the contributions (employers and employees). The Employers’ 
contribution works out to about 4.35% of the wages. The employees’ contribution is 2.17% of the wages. The expenditure on medical 
care is shared between the Employees’ State Insurance Corporation and the State Governments in the agreed ratio of 7 . 1 . The Corporation 
does not receive any financial assistance from the Central Government. 

EXTENSION OF THE SCHEME DURING 1977-78 ; 

6. During the period 1st November, 1976, to 31st October, 1977, the Scheme has been extended to cover 1 . 12 lakhs more employees 
in 14 centre.s. Medical care was also extended to 1.33 lakhs more family (insured-persons) units. The total coverage of employees under 
the Scheme as on 31st October, 1977, stood at 55.26 lakhs in 410 centres. The total number of beneficiaries for medical benefit is 2.32 
lakhs inclusive of insured persons and their family members. 

7. The duration of sickness benefit payable under the Act has been enhanced with effect from 1-5-1977 from 56 days to 91 day.s in any 
two consecutive benefit periods bringing it at par with the minimum standards laid down by the I.L.O. for developing countries. The 
Permanent Disablement Benefit and Dependants’ Benefit pensions payable in cases of permanently disabled or deceased insured pcr.sons 
who suffered disablement or death on or before 31-3-1975 have also been increased from 1st October, to the following extent, to compensate 
them for the increase in the cost of living index. 

(a) Cases where disablement or death occurred on or before 20% of the amount subject to the rounding off to the next higher 

31-3-1974 multiple of 5 paise, 

(b) Cases where disablement or death occurred between 10% of the amount subject to the rounding off to the next higher 

1-4-1974 and 31*3-1975 multiple of 5 paise.

11,2 The expenditure on this activity is intitially incurred by the State Governments who are in administrative control of the 
Medical Scheme except in the Union Territory of D;lhf. The Corporation pays its share on quarterly basis on receipt of expenditure 
statement from the State Governments. In order to ensure effective control, the Corpoiation has fixed ceilings on medical expenditure 
under the different categories of Medical Care and any expenditure incurred by the State Govermnents over and above these ceilings is 
borne exclusively by them and such e,\cess expenditure is not reflected in the Corporation budget. 

The ceilings for expenditure on medical care are as follow.s : —

The provision in the Revised Estimates 1977-78 takes Into account the following ;• — 

(a) Rs. 1,76,00 lakhs and Rs. 1,04.00 lakhs transferred to Permanent Disablement Benefit and Dependants’ Benefit Reserve Fnnd.s 
respectively, as one time adjustment cost (in the form of capitalised value) on account of increase in rates with effect from 1-10-977. 
fb) The figure takes into account the write back of Rs.5,48.24 lakhs and Rs. 62.34 lakhs from the amounts in the Permanent Dis- 
ablement Benefit and Dependents’ Benefit Reserve Funds, respectively. The Vainer in Fifth Qninqpenpial Valuation of the 
Employees’ State Insurance Corporation as on 3Ist March, 1974, has assessed those amounts as surplus credit to the Funds. 

1 2.2 The cligiblity for different categories of Cush Benefits is dependent on the number of contributions paid by the employees and 
the rate of their wages. Roughly, the Cash Benefit on account of sickness comes to 50% of the wages, in case of Disablement and De- 
pendants’ Benefit it work.s out to 62.5% of the wages and roughly full wages are paid in the case of Maternity Benefit to female insured 
workers. Funcraf Benefit is paid at the rate of Rs. 100/-in the event of death of an insured persen, 

12.3 These beneflt.s are paid to the Insured Persons or their beneficiaries directly by the Corporation through its Local/Pay Office® 
which are Ippatcd in almost al) fhe indu.sfrial centres where the Scheme has been implemented. The incidence of expenditure on ca.sh 
benefits depends on a variety of factors, e.g. State of health, industrial peftce and the awareness of tl e workers about their entitlement to the 
benefits etc. It is therefore, not possible to fix any plysical targets.

3100 THE G.\ZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 27. 1979/KARTIKA 5, 1901 [Part II— Sec. .IfiOJ 


16.2 Under the Employees’ State Insurance Act 1948, the administration of the medical scheme is the statutory responsibility of the 
State Governments. As such it is for them to provide necessary buildings to house the Employees' State Insurance Hospitals/Dispen- 
saries. In the initial stages, the Corporation had sizeable balance of income over expenditure, whereas the State Governments were not 
finding adequate resources to construct necessary buildings and consequently the Scheme was not making 'much headway. The Corpora- 
tion, therefore, decided to invest available surplus of its income over expenditure in the construction of buildings for housing Its own offices 
and Employees’ State Insurance Hospitals/Dispensaries. 


16.3 The Corporation in its meetings held on 2nd February and 3rd December, 1974, reviewed the Capital construction programme 
of the Employees’ State Insurance projects in various States and approved that — 


fi) 10% of the total revenue derived from ‘Contributions’ be credited to the Capital construction Reserve Fund and expenditure on 
construction of Hospitals/Dispensaries/Other medical institutions and office buildings/stalT quarters be incurred in the ratio of 
8 : 2 , 

(ii) Plans and estimates for additional hospitals/annexcs may be sanctioned so as to provide upto 5 beds per thousand employees 
as approved at the time of sanctioning new projects. Normally, the limit of 4 beds per thousand employees as already fixed shall 
not be exceeded and only in exceptional case., the additional beds will be sanctioned after ensuring that there is acute necessity 
for additional beds depending upon the incidence of diseases requiring hospitalisation based on the occupancy of beds in the 
existing ESI Hospitals in that area, 

(iii) For purposes of securing land, additional employees as are likely to be covered in the next 2 years as on the date of sanction of 
the proposal may also be taken into account for working out the number of beds admissible. Plans and estimates for construc- 
tion of additional beds may however, be sanctioned only after notification has been issued by the State Government under 
section 1(5) of the E.S.I. Act. 

(iv) Plans and estimates for construction of other buildings such a.s dispensaries, specialist centres, offices for the Administrative 
Medical Officers, Medical stores etc. may bo sanctioned on merits of each case without any limitations on per capita basis, 
because expenditure on such works is of minor character. 

16.4 The yardstick for provision of hospital beds has normally been fixed at the rate of 4 beds per one thousand family employees 
units. According to this yardstick 22,000 beds are required to cater to the needs of the entire Insured population, 

16.5 The Capital expenditure on the acquisition of sites and construction of buildings for Administrative Offices, dispensaries and 
hospitals including staff quarters upto the end of 1976-77 amounted to Rs. 53,69.27 lakhs (including loans of Rs. 3,62. IS lakhs 
sanctioned to Maharashtra State for construction of hopsitals). 

16.6 The Corporation has upto 31st October, 1977, constructed and commissioned 62 hospitals and 24 annexes having bed capacity 
of 13,809 beds. In addition 177 dispensary buildings have also been constructed and commissioned 23 hospitals (3,936 beds), 18 annexes 
(354 beds) and 34 dispensary buildings are under construction. For capital construction, outlay of Rs.72,30. 92 lakhs has been sanctioned 
upto 31st October, 1977. Programme of construction works (including works in progress) is given in the Annexure-Il.
